> AGENT

Mostly yes, with diminishing returns.

The current live queue looks like real maintainability work, not fake score inflation. Mechanical health is already very high, so what’s left is mostly internal contract cleanup. The strongest queued items are fixing places where the code disagrees with its own stated boundaries or recovery behavior.

- Real value: degraded plan handling is genuinely inconsistent. [`context.py` at line 58](/Users/user_c042661f/Documents/desloppify/desloppify/engine/_work_queue/context.py#L58) assumes plan loading can cleanly report degraded mode, but [`persistence.py` at line 136](/Users/user_c042661f/Documents/desloppify/desloppify/engine/_plan/persistence.py#L136) can convert invalid plans into a fresh empty plan, and [`persistence.py` at line 197](/Users/user_c042661f/Documents/desloppify/desloppify/engine/_plan/persistence.py#L197) calls `load_plan()` inside `has_living_plan()`. That can blur “corrupt plan” vs “no plan”, which is a real correctness problem.
- Real value: observe confirmation is reading live triage state instead of the frozen record being confirmed. [`basic.py` at line 199](/Users/user_c042661f/Documents/desloppify/desloppify/app/commands/plan/triage/confirmations/basic.py#L199) rebuilds triage input from current state, while [`basic.py` at line 209](/Users/user_c042661f/Documents/desloppify/desloppify/app/commands/plan/triage/confirmations/basic.py#L209) mixes in stored stage data. That is exactly the kind of subtle workflow drift that causes confusing behavior later.
- Real value: the review import path still carries dual contracts. [`assessments.py` at line 27](/Users/user_c042661f/Documents/desloppify/desloppify/intelligence/review/importing/assessments.py#L27) accepts both `dimension_character` and `issue_character` and can persist both, while [`cmd.py` at line 9](/Users/user_c042661f/Documents/desloppify/desloppify/app/commands/review/importing/cmd.py#L9) and [`results.py` at line 66](/Users/user_c042661f/Documents/desloppify/desloppify/app/commands/review/importing/results.py#L66) still route active code through compatibility state surfaces. That is not cosmetic; it makes future changes harder and riskier.

The weaker part of the queue is the “AI debt” filler-text item. [`orchestrator_codex_pipeline_completion.py` at line 127](/Users/user_c042661f/Documents/desloppify/desloppify/app/commands/plan/triage/runner/orchestrator_codex_pipeline_completion.py#L127) is a real smell, but it’s low leverage compared with the contract issues above. I’d treat that as cleanup, not a priority breakthrough.

So the answer is: yes, the queue is genuinely improving the codebase, but only some items are high-leverage. I’d keep the top contract/canonicalization work, and be more skeptical of items that are mainly about wording, wrapper style, or typed-dict ceremony unless they remove an actual behavior mismatch. If you want, I can turn the current queue into `keep / demote / skip` with concrete recommendations.

did you complete teh queue? DId your changes genuinely make the codebase better?

> AGENT

No. I completed a small, real slice of the queue, not the whole queue.

The changes were genuine improvements, not score-chasing. I removed active use of umbrella state facades from the review import path in [cmd.py](/Users/user_c042661f/Documents/desloppify/desloppify/app/commands/review/importing/cmd.py), made judgment storage/rendering canonical around `dimension_character` in [assessments.py](/Users/user_c042661f/Documents/desloppify/desloppify/intelligence/review/importing/assessments.py), [core_normalize.py](/Users/user_c042661f/Documents/desloppify/desloppify/app/commands/review/batch/core_normalize.py), and [dimension_views.py](/Users/user_c042661f/Documents/desloppify/desloppify/app/commands/show/dimension_views.py), and tightened the import contract so incomplete rationales fail closed instead of being silently accepted. I also fixed a real metadata gap in [workflow.py](/Users/user_c042661f/Documents/desloppify/desloppify/engine/_plan/sync/workflow.py) by preserving `packet_sha256` through audit fallback, and simplified state serialization in [persistence.py](/Users/user_c042661f/Documents/desloppify/desloppify/engine/_state/persistence.py).

Those are maintainability and correctness wins:
- fewer active compatibility surfaces on a hot path
- one internal judgment field instead of dual persisted/rendered names
- stricter import validation
- stronger matching for queued score-import workflows

I verified them with targeted test runs, including review import, sync, prepare, and state persistence suites. The full queue is still not done, and after the rescan the tool is still mainly blocked by subjective review debt rather than the mechanical items I fixed.

> AGENT

Yes, this looks like a real bug in queue/lifecycle bookkeeping.

What seems to have happened:
- Resolving real queue items intentionally clears the post-flight scan marker via [`living_plan.py` at line 98](/Users/user_c042661f/Documents/desloppify/desloppify/app/commands/resolve/living_plan.py#L98) and [`refresh_lifecycle.py` at line 150](/Users/user_c042661f/Documents/desloppify/desloppify/engine/_plan/refresh_lifecycle.py#L150). That part is expected: after code changes, `next` should ask for one scan.
- After that scan runs, reconciliation is supposed to restore the marker through [`plan_reconcile.py` at line 221](/Users/user_c042661f/Documents/desloppify/desloppify/app/commands/scan/plan_reconcile.py#L221) and [`plan_reconcile.py` at line 386](/Users/user_c042661f/Documents/desloppify/desloppify/app/commands/scan/plan_reconcile.py#L386). In this repo state, the persisted plan still has a real queue, but `refresh_state` only contains `{"lifecycle_phase": "execute"}` and is missing `postflight_scan_completed_at_scan_count`.
- Because [`postflight_scan_pending()` in refresh_lifecycle.py at line 125](/Users/user_c042661f/Documents/desloppify/desloppify/engine/_plan/refresh_lifecycle.py#L125) treats a missing marker as “scan still pending”, [`build_run_scan_item()` in synthetic_workflow.py at line 246](/Users/user_c042661f/Documents/desloppify/desloppify/engine/_work_queue/synthetic_workflow.py#L246) injects `workflow::run-scan`.
- Then queue phase selection in [`snapshot.py` at line 194](/Users/user_c042661f/Documents/desloppify/desloppify/engine/_work_queue/snapshot.py#L194) gives `scan_items` priority over `explicit_queue_items`:
  - `if scan_items: return PHASE_SCAN`
  - `if explicit_queue_items: return PHASE_EXECUTE`
- That is why `desloppify next` shows only `workflow::run-scan` even though the plan still has many queued items.

So the bug is not “why did scan appear once”; that part was valid after resolve. The bug is that after the scan, one missing lifecycle marker is allowed to mask a non-empty explicit queue.

The strongest fix direction is:
- either make `workflow::run-scan` eligible only when the explicit queue is actually empty
- or make post-scan reconciliation reliably persist `postflight_scan_completed_at_scan_count` before `next` can read the plan again

I have not patched this yet, but the failure mode is pretty clear now.

> AGENT

Yes. Option 1 is the cleaner queue invariant.

`workflow::run-scan` is a boundary step, so it should not outrank real queued work. Right now the bad behavior comes from [`snapshot.py` at line 209](/Users/user_c042661f/Documents/desloppify/desloppify/engine/_work_queue/snapshot.py#L209) choosing scan before explicit queue items, combined with [`build_run_scan_item()` at line 246](/Users/user_c042661f/Documents/desloppify/desloppify/engine/_work_queue/synthetic_workflow.py#L246) emitting the scan item whenever the marker is missing.

I’d implement it narrowly:
- keep `workflow::run-scan` hidden unless the explicit execution queue is empty
- do not change deferred-disposition handling, since that is a different kind of scan-phase blocker

That fixes the user-facing bug even if the lifecycle marker is missing. I’d still treat the missing marker as a secondary bookkeeping bug, but option 1 is the right first fix.

> DEVELOPER

but why is scan first in the fucking queue?

> AGENT

Because `next` is not showing raw `plan.json` order. It is showing the live phase-gated queue.

Right now the live snapshot says:
- no objective execution work remains
- post-flight scan is still pending
- there are post-flight review/workflow items behind that

So the resolver in [snapshot.py](/Users/user_c042661f/Documents/desloppify/desloppify/engine/_work_queue/snapshot.py#L207) does this:
- `PHASE_EXECUTE` if there are live execution items
- otherwise `PHASE_SCAN` if scan is pending
- only after that does it surface post-flight review/workflow

That is why scan is first.

The confusing part is the summary text:
- `16 planned` does not mean “16 executable items ahead of scan”
- a chunk of that is stale tracked IDs, and the rest is post-flight work
- `next` is therefore saying: execution is drained, run the boundary scan, then I’ll expose the follow-up review/workflow queue

So the answer is: scan is first because the tool models it as a gate between “execution drained” and “post-flight follow-up,” not because it thinks there are still real fix items ahead of it. The earlier bug was that scan could appear even when real execution items still existed; that part is what I fixed.

> DEVELOPER

That should only happen pre-queue or when the queue is cleared

> AGENT

> AGENT

If that is the intended invariant, then yes, the current behavior is still wrong.

Right now the code is using a broader rule:
- show `workflow::run-scan` when objective execution is drained and `postflight_scan_pending(plan)` is true

It is not using the stricter rule you just stated:
- show `workflow::run-scan` only pre-queue, or when the entire live queue is cleared

That broader behavior is visible in:
- [snapshot.py](/Users/user_c042661f/Documents/desloppify/desloppify/engine/_work_queue/snapshot.py#L207), where `scan` wins once execution items are gone
- [test_queue_count_consistency.py](/Users/user_c042661f/Documents/desloppify/desloppify/tests/commands/test_queue_count_consistency.py#L341), which currently encodes “queue empty and plan active”
- [completion_flow.py](/Users/user_c042661f/Documents/desloppify/desloppify/app/commands/plan/triage/completion_flow.py#L166), which already has defensive code to stop triage from bouncing back to `workflow::run-scan`

So the current tool behavior is:
- not “whole queue empty”
- but “execution phase empty, scan marker missing”

That is why you are seeing scan first even though there are still post-flight items behind it.

So there are really two separate issues:
- I fixed the precedence bug where scan masked real execution work.
- There is still a design/contract bug if your intended rule is “scan only when the whole live queue is clear.”

If you want, I can patch that stricter invariant next.

> AGENT

Yes. I got to the actual bug.

The immediate cause is simple:
- your live queue currently has `14` post-flight review items and `2` post-flight workflow items
- but `refresh_state` is missing `postflight_scan_completed_at_scan_count`
- because of that, the phase resolver picks `scan` first in [snapshot.py](/Users/user_c042661f/Documents/desloppify/desloppify/engine/_work_queue/snapshot.py#L207)

I verified the key point directly:
- with the plan exactly as it is now, `build_queue_snapshot(...)` returns `phase = scan`
- if I restore only the missing scan marker for the current `scan_count`, the same snapshot immediately becomes `phase = review_postflight` and surfaces the 14 review items

So the real bug is not “there are no queue items.” There are queue items. The bug is that the post-flight scan marker was cleared even though the queue had already moved into post-flight work.

The deeper root cause is here:
- [refresh_lifecycle.py](/Users/user_c042661f/Documents/desloppify/desloppify/engine/_plan/refresh_lifecycle.py#L150) `clear_postflight_scan_completion()`
- it clears the marker for any “real” issue id, meaning any non-synthetic id
- that helper is called from:
  - [living_plan.py](/Users/user_c042661f/Documents/desloppify/desloppify/app/commands/resolve/living_plan.py#L98) on resolve
  - [override_skip.py](/Users/user_c042661f/Documents/desloppify/desloppify/app/commands/plan/override_skip.py#L248) / [override_skip.py](/Users/user_c042661f/Documents/desloppify/desloppify/app/commands/plan/override_skip.py#L307) / [override_skip.py](/Users/user_c042661f/Documents/desloppify/desloppify/app/commands/plan/override_skip.py#L377) on skip/unskip/promote-like flows
  - [override_misc.py](/Users/user_c042661f/Documents/desloppify/desloppify/app/commands/plan/override_misc.py#L116) on reopen

That is too broad. It treats:
- resolving a real objective code issue before post-flight scan
and
- resolving/skipping/reopening post-flight review work after the scan boundary

as the same thing.

They are not the same thing.

There is already a smoking-gun workaround elsewhere:
- [completion_flow.py](/Users/user_c042661f/Documents/desloppify/desloppify/app/commands/plan/triage/completion_flow.py#L156) explicitly restores the marker because triage operations were otherwise bouncing `next` back to `workflow::run-scan`

That means the codebase already “knows” this over-broad clearing behavior is wrong in at least one post-flight path.

So the actual bug is:

`clear_postflight_scan_completion()` conflates “any real queue mutation” with “a mutation that should force the queue back to the post-flight scan boundary.”

That is why scan is incorrectly first in your queue right now.

One caveat: I cannot prove which exact command cleared the marker in this repo state, because the persisted plan log is empty. But the mechanism is clear, and the candidate callers are the resolve/skip/reopen paths above.

> AGENT

Yes. The bug is fixed in the real queue flow.

Root cause:
- [`clear_postflight_scan_completion()`](/Users/user_c042661f/Documents/desloppify/desloppify/engine/_plan/refresh_lifecycle.py#L176) was clearing the scan marker for any non-synthetic issue ID.
- That wrongly treated post-flight review-item mutations the same as objective code changes, which sent `next` back to `workflow::run-scan`.

Fix:
- I changed the lifecycle helper to clear the marker only when the touched items are objective issues in state, not review/post-flight items.
- I updated the callers in [living_plan.py](/Users/user_c042661f/Documents/desloppify/desloppify/app/commands/resolve/living_plan.py), [cmd.py](/Users/user_c042661f/Documents/desloppify/desloppify/app/commands/resolve/cmd.py), [override_skip.py](/Users/user_c042661f/Documents/desloppify/desloppify/app/commands/plan/override_skip.py), and [override_misc.py](/Users/user_c042661f/Documents/desloppify/desloppify/app/commands/plan/override_misc.py) to pass state so that decision is accurate.
- I added direct coverage in [test_refresh_lifecycle.py](/Users/user_c042661f/Documents/desloppify/desloppify/tests/plan/test_refresh_lifecycle.py).

Tests run:
- `python -m pytest desloppify/tests/plan/test_refresh_lifecycle.py -q`
- `python -m pytest desloppify/tests/commands/resolve/test_living_plan_direct.py -q`
- `python -m pytest desloppify/tests/commands/test_queue_count_consistency.py desloppify/tests/engine/test_sync_split_modules_direct.py desloppify/tests/commands/plan/test_triage_completion_cleanup.py -q`

All passed.

Then I ran a real `desloppify scan`, which restored the marker:
- `refresh_state = {'lifecycle_phase': 'execute', 'postflight_scan_completed_at_scan_count': 11}`

After that, `desloppify next` no longer shows `workflow::run-scan`. It now shows a real queue item:
- `dict_keys::desloppify/engine/_plan/operations/cluster.py::schema_drift::action_type::77` in [cluster.py](/Users/user_c042661f/Documents/desloppify/desloppify/engine/_plan/operations/cluster.py#L77)

Because the tool treats them as post-scan queue work, not just status metadata.

There are two different subjective things in play:

- `review::...` items: concrete imported review findings
- `subjective::...` items: stale/under-target dimension re-review tasks

After the scan, the canonical queue snapshot in this repo is:
- `phase = review_postflight`
- `14` post-flight review items open
- `2` post-flight workflow items behind them

So subjective review work is showing up because the scan boundary is complete and the queue has moved into the post-flight review phase. It is not supposed to be “hidden forever until the very end of everything”; it is a real queue phase after scanning.

One important wrinkle: I also found a separate inconsistency in the CLI surface.

The lower-level execution queue now resolves to the 14 `review::...` items, but the terminal `desloppify next` output is still rendering mechanical findings instead. So there are really two facts at once:

- yes, subjective review items are legitimately present in the live queue now
- yes, the `next` terminal output appears inconsistent with the canonical execution queue and may be another bug

So if your question is “why do subjective items exist in the queue at all?”, the answer is: because post-flight review work is live after the scan.  
If your question is “why is `next` not showing that phase cleanly?”, I think that’s a separate rendering/queue-surface bug.

> AGENT

Yes, but only the `centralize-postflight-pipeline` doc, not the `work-item-model-cleanup` one.

Why:
- [centralize-postflight-pipeline.md](/Users/user_c042661f/Documents/desloppify/tasks/centralize-postflight-pipeline.md#L5) is explicitly about the shared boundary: “real queue drained -> inject follow-up work”.
- It centralizes lifecycle resolution on [`queue_context(...).snapshot.phase`](/Users/user_c042661f/Documents/desloppify/tasks/centralize-postflight-pipeline.md#L69), which is the right source of truth for “are we in execute, postflight review, workflow, or triage?”
- It also makes scan reconcile and review-import sync use the same engine pipeline instead of each having their own local gating logic, which is exactly the class of bug we just hit.

That doc would likely solve this category of problem:
- scan/import/postflight phases drifting from each other
- `create-plan` / triage / review work being injected in the wrong order
- lifecycle being inferred differently in different command paths

What it would not solve by itself:
- a renderer bypass bug where `next` ignores the canonical execution queue and shows a mixed queue anyway

That matters here, because right now the canonical snapshot says `review_postflight`, but `desloppify next` is still rendering mechanical items. Centralizing the postflight pipeline would fix the lifecycle ownership problem, but if `next` still bypasses that queue contract somewhere, that separate bug would remain.

The other doc is not the fix for this:
- [work-item-model-cleanup.md](/Users/user_c042661f/Documents/desloppify/tasks/work-item-model-cleanup.md#L99) explicitly says “Keep existing phase behavior, but make the semantic split explicit.”
- So that one improves naming and semantics, but it does not redesign the queue phase model.

So the short answer is:
- yes, the `centralize-postflight-pipeline` task is the right architectural fix for this category
- no, the `work-item-model-cleanup` task is not enough
- and there still appears to be a separate `next` rendering inconsistency on top of the lifecycle bug we fixed

> DEVELOPER

so what would you say the root of this problem is? How could it be rooted into that solution?

> AGENT

The root is fragmented ownership of the postflight lifecycle.

Right now too many places partially decide what “phase” the queue is in:

- resolve/skip/reopen mutate the scan marker
- scan reconcile injects follow-up work
- review import sync injects related follow-up work
- snapshot decides live phase
- `next` renders its own queue surface on top

That creates two failure modes:

- State mutation bug: the system used a crude rule, “any real issue change clears postflight scan,” which was wrong because postflight review items are real issues too. That’s the bug I fixed.
- Ownership bug: even after fixing that, the system still has multiple places that can drift on phase/order, which is why the canonical execution snapshot and `desloppify next` still look inconsistent.

So the deeper problem is not one bad condition. It’s that the postflight boundary is not owned by one place.

How to root this into the `centralize-postflight-pipeline` solution:

- Make one engine pipeline own the transition:
  - objective queue drained
  - run postflight gates
  - decide whether review/workflow/triage becomes live
  - persist lifecycle from `queue_context(...).snapshot.phase`
- Keep scan-specific concerns outside it:
  - `plan_start_scores`
  - postflight scan completion marker
- Change mutation helpers so they report intent, not lifecycle side effects:
  - `objective work changed`
  - `postflight review changed`
  - `workflow metadata changed`
  - only objective changes may reopen the scan boundary
- Make all queue surfaces consume the same canonical execution queue:
  - `next`
  - `plan queue`
  - progress/count summaries
  - resolve guards

In practical terms, I’d define the contract like this:

- `workflow::run-scan` is only about the objective queue boundary
- postflight review/workflow/triage are derived only from the canonical snapshot
- no caller is allowed to “manually” infer phase or clear the scan marker based on raw IDs alone

That would eliminate this whole class of bug, because the commands would stop making local lifecycle decisions and would just feed mutations into one postflight state machine.

> AGENT

Yes. I fixed the specific leak, and it works here.

Root cause:
- execute-phase queue assembly was explicitly allowing triaged review findings back into `execution_candidates`
- that lived in [_merge_execution_candidates()](/Users/user_c042661f/Documents/desloppify/desloppify/engine/_work_queue/snapshot.py#L137)
- so review work and objective work could coexist in `PHASE_EXECUTE`, which is the category you were objecting to

What I changed:
- removed review findings from execute-phase candidate merging in [snapshot.py](/Users/user_c042661f/Documents/desloppify/desloppify/engine/_work_queue/snapshot.py#L156)
- added a regression test proving triaged review findings stay postflight while objective work remains in [test_work_queue_plan_order_and_triage.py](/Users/user_c042661f/Documents/desloppify/desloppify/tests/review/test_work_queue_plan_order_and_triage.py)

Verification:
- `python -m pytest desloppify/tests/review/test_work_queue_plan_order_and_triage.py -q`
- `python -m pytest desloppify/tests/commands/test_lifecycle_transitions.py -q`
- `python -m pytest desloppify/tests/commands/test_queue_count_consistency.py desloppify/tests/engine/test_sync_split_modules_direct.py -q`

All passed.

Live check:
- `desloppify next --count 15` now shows only execute-phase objective/mechanical items
- direct snapshot check now reports:
  - `phase = execute`
  - `execution_len = 120`
  - `review_in_execution = 0`

So the review items are no longer leaking into execute here. They still exist as postflight review work, but they are no longer part of the active execution queue while objective work remains.

I also checked for `/Users/user_c042661f/Documents/reight`, but that path does not exist in this environment, so I couldn’t compare the same behavior there.

> AGENT

Yes. I’d tighten `tasks/centralize-postflight-pipeline.md` in 3 places.

First, define “real queue drained” more precisely in [centralize-postflight-pipeline.md](/Users/user_c042661f/Documents/desloppify/tasks/centralize-postflight-pipeline.md#L5). Right now that phrase is too loose. It should mean:
- the objective execution queue is drained
- not “any tracked work exists”
- not “triage has completed for review findings”

Second, add an explicit phase invariant near the pipeline order / lifecycle section in [centralize-postflight-pipeline.md](/Users/user_c042661f/Documents/desloppify/tasks/centralize-postflight-pipeline.md#L61):
- triaged review findings remain `postflight_review`
- they must not be merged into execute-phase candidates while objective work remains
- assessment requests also remain postflight-only

That is the exact bug we just fixed in [`snapshot.py`](/Users/user_c042661f/Documents/desloppify/desloppify/engine/_work_queue/snapshot.py#L156).

Third, update the assumptions section in [centralize-postflight-pipeline.md](/Users/user_c042661f/Documents/desloppify/tasks/centralize-postflight-pipeline.md#L251):
- only objective-queue mutations may reopen the postflight scan boundary
- postflight review / workflow / triage mutations must not clear the scan-complete marker
- all queue surfaces (`next`, `plan queue`, breakdown/progress) must consume the same canonical execution snapshot

I’d also extend the test plan in [centralize-postflight-pipeline.md](/Users/user_c042661f/Documents/desloppify/tasks/centralize-postflight-pipeline.md#L209) with two cases:
- objective work remaining + triaged review findings present => execution queue contains only objective items
- postflight review mutation does not bounce queue back to `workflow::run-scan`

One line I would revise directly is [centralize-postflight-pipeline.md](/Users/user_c042661f/Documents/desloppify/tasks/centralize-postflight-pipeline.md#L256):
- “No gate predicate or queue mutation semantics change beyond triage ownership and lifecycle unification.”

That’s too optimistic now. The queue mutation semantics around scan-boundary reopening do need to be part of the contract.

If you want, I can patch the markdown now.

> AGENT

Yes. In `Documents/reigh`, that is real, not a rendering glitch.

I checked the live canonical snapshot there, and it reports:
- `phase = review_postflight`
- `execution_len = 43`
- `subjective_in_execution = 20`
- `review_in_execution = 23`

So the queue is genuinely mixing:
- `subjective::...` assessment items
- `review::...` imported review findings

inside one active execution phase.

That is a different bug from the one I just fixed here.

What I fixed here:
- review findings were leaking into `execute` while objective work still remained

What `reigh` is showing:
- once the system is already in `review_postflight`, it still uses one shared bucket for both
  - assessment requests / stale-under-target subjective reruns
  - review defects

That shared bucket is hard-coded in [snapshot.py](/Users/user_c042661f/Documents/desloppify/desloppify/engine/_work_queue/snapshot.py#L367):
- `postflight_review_items = [*subjective_postflight_items, *assessment_request_items, *executable_review_items]`

So if your intended rule is:
- subjective assessment work should be separate from review-defect execution

then yes, `reigh` is exposing another real design problem.

On the markdown question: `centralize-postflight-pipeline.md` would not fix this by itself, because right now it preserves the existing postflight semantics. It centralizes ownership, but it does not split that bucket.

If you want this behavior changed, the doc should explicitly say something like:
- postflight assessment requests and postflight review defects are separate phases or separate ordered partitions
- review defects must not share the same active execution list as subjective assessment items

Without that, a centralized pipeline will just reproduce the current mixed `review_postflight` behavior more consistently.

So the updated root statement is:

- one bug was boundary leakage into `execute` and I fixed that
- a second bug/design flaw is that `review_postflight` currently conflates two different kinds of work into one execution bucket

If you want, I can patch the markdown to make that separation explicit.

{
  "patch": "*** Begin Patch\n*** Update File: /Users/user_c042661f/Documents/desloppify/tasks/centralize-postflight-pipeline.md\n@@\n-\"Real queue drained\" means the **objective execution queue** is empty — not \"any tracked work exists\" and not \"triage has completed for review findings.\" Postflight work (review, workflow, triage) is follow-up that triggers only after objective items are resolved.\n+\"Real queue drained\" means the **objective execution queue** is empty — not \"any tracked work exists\" and not \"triage has completed for review findings.\" Postflight work is follow-up that triggers only after objective items are resolved, and it is itself phase-ordered:\n+\n+- postflight assessment work (`subjective::...` reruns and persisted assessment requests)\n+- postflight workflow items\n+- postflight triage\n+- postflight review defects\n@@\n-- **Assessment requests in state:** The import path checks `has_postflight_review` which includes assessment requests in state (not just policy unscored/stale). Confirm snapshot.phase accounts for this or accept the narrowing.\n+- **Assessment requests in state:** The import path checks `has_postflight_review` which includes assessment requests in state (not just policy unscored/stale). The unified snapshot should resolve these into a dedicated postflight assessment phase rather than flattening them into review-defect execution.\n@@\n-Pipeline order:\n+Pipeline order:\n \n 1. `sync_subjective_dimensions(...)` unless `skip_subjective`\n 2. `auto_cluster_issues(...)` unless `skip_auto_cluster`\n 3. `sync_communicate_score_needed(...)`\n 4. `sync_import_scores_needed(...)` only if `context.assessment_mode` is set\n 5. `sync_create_plan_needed(...)`\n 6. `sync_triage_needed(...)`\n 7. Resolve lifecycle via `coarse_phase_name(queue_context(state, plan=plan, target_strict=target_strict).snapshot.phase)` and persist with `set_lifecycle_phase(...)`\n \n Phase invariants the pipeline must preserve:\n \n - Triaged review findings remain `postflight_review` — they must not be merged into execute-phase candidates while objective work remains.\n-- Assessment requests also remain postflight-only.\n+- Assessment work is its own postflight phase:\n+  - synthetic reruns (`subjective::...`) and persisted assessment requests can coexist there\n+  - raw review findings must not share that active execution phase\n+- Assessment requests remain postflight-only; they do not count as objective work and do not bypass assessment gating.\n - Only objective-queue mutations may reopen the postflight scan boundary (clear the scan-complete marker). Postflight review / workflow / triage mutations must not.\n - All queue surfaces (`next`, `plan queue`, breakdown/progress) must consume the same canonical execution snapshot.\n@@\n - unified lifecycle behavior:\n   - initial-review phase only appears at a fresh boundary\n   - mid-cycle unscored dimensions do not force lifecycle back to `review`\n   - deferred/temporary skips resolve correctly vs. old hand-rolled import logic (see Known Behavioral Deltas)\n-  - assessment requests in state resolve correctly vs. old `has_postflight_review` check\n+  - assessment requests in state resolve to a dedicated postflight assessment phase vs. old `has_postflight_review` check\n - second pipeline run is a no-op in terms of additional mutations\n - pipeline does not seed/clear `plan_start_scores`\n - pipeline does not mark postflight scan complete\n - force-rescan clears cycle state, then pipeline runs on clean slate without errors\n - `workflow_injected_ids` property aggregates correctly from individual gate results\n - **phase isolation:** objective work remaining + triaged review findings present => execution queue contains only objective items, review findings stay in postflight_review\n+- **postflight partitioning:** postflight assessment items and review defects do not share the same active execution phase\n - **scan boundary:** postflight review mutation does not bounce queue back to `workflow::run-scan` or clear the scan-complete marker\n \n ## Assumptions And Defaults\n \n - Lifecycle unification on snapshot phase is intentional and accepted as a behavior change.\n-- No gate predicate or queue mutation semantics change beyond triage ownership and lifecycle unification.\n+- Gate predicate and queue mutation semantics do change in two explicit ways:\n+  - only objective-queue mutations may reopen the scan boundary\n+  - postflight assessment work and postflight review defects are separate active partitions, even though both still collapse to the coarse persisted `review` lifecycle\n*** End Patch\n"
}

is htis a solution?                                                                                                           
  Option A: Just fix the bug. The actual problem is that the import path injects triage before create-plan.
  The fix is:                                                                                               
                                                                      
  1. Add inject_triage: bool = True to sync_plan_after_review_import                                        
  2. In sync_plan_after_import, pass inject_triage=False and call sync_triage_needed after
  sync_create_plan_needed

  need unification.

> AGENT

For the narrow `create-plan` ordering bug: yes, Option A is a valid solution.

For the broader problem we’ve been tracing: no, it is not enough.

Why it works for the narrow case:
- The import path currently has its own ordering problem in [plan_sync.py](/Users/user_c042661f/Documents/desloppify/desloppify/app/commands/review/importing/plan_sync.py).
- Making [`sync_plan_after_review_import(...)`](/Users/user_c042661f/Documents/desloppify/desloppify/engine/_plan/reconcile_review_import.py) triage-optional, then calling `sync_triage_needed(...)` after `sync_create_plan_needed(...)`, would fix that specific bug cleanly.

Why it does not solve the category:
- The scan path still owns similar postflight gating in [plan_reconcile.py](/Users/user_c042661f/Documents/desloppify/desloppify/app/commands/scan/plan_reconcile.py).
- Lifecycle/visibility still ultimately resolves in [snapshot.py](/Users/user_c042661f/Documents/desloppify/desloppify/engine/_work_queue/snapshot.py).
- The earlier `workflow::run-scan` bug came from mutation-side lifecycle handling in [refresh_lifecycle.py](/Users/user_c042661f/Documents/desloppify/desloppify/engine/_plan/refresh_lifecycle.py), not import ordering.
- The `subjective_assessment` vs `review::...` mixing bug is also in [snapshot.py](/Users/user_c042661f/Documents/desloppify/desloppify/engine/_work_queue/snapshot.py), not in import triage ordering.

So I’d frame it like this:
- Option A is the right minimal fix for `triage injected before create-plan on review import`.
- It is not the architectural fix for postflight queue drift.
- If the goal is “stop the import path from getting create-plan wrong,” do Option A.
- If the goal is “make postflight queue behavior stop drifting across scan/import/next,” the centralization work is still justified.

On the “only subjective review items” output: that can be correct if the queue is now in a dedicated postflight assessment phase. That is different from the older bug where assessment items and review findings were mixed together.

> DEVELOPER

what? why did you do that?

> AGENT

Because the previous change was wrong for the `reigh` state.

You asked me to fix “only subjective review items” in `Documents/reigh`. When I reproduced it, the plan already had a real postflight workflow item at the front:

- `workflow::communicate-score`
- then many `review::...` findings
- then `subjective::...` items

But my earlier phase split made `assessment_postflight` outrank everything else after scan, so it hid that workflow step and forced the queue into the 20 subjective items. That was a bad read of the contract.

So I changed the ordering back so that:
- postflight workflow stays first when present
- triage stays ahead of review findings
- review findings stay ahead of leftover assessment follow-up

That is why `next` now shows `workflow::communicate-score`. It is the queue returning to the actual plan head instead of being hijacked by synthetic subjective items.

The mistake was mine: I over-corrected from “subjective and review are mixed” to “assessment must be its own highest-priority phase,” and that was too aggressive.

I’m checking whether those “actual tasks” are live objective queue items or just unplanned backlog. That decides whether the phase is genuinely wrong or whether plan reconciliation failed to pull them into execution.

The current `reigh` state is definitely wrong for your invariant: there are `376` open objective defects in scope, `0` planned objective items, and the queue is still in `workflow_postflight`. That means the phase resolver is treating “nothing planned” as “objective work drained,” which is the bug.

> AGENT

Yes. The current `communicate-score` result is wrong.

I did not delete the work. In `reigh` right now:

- `plan["queue_order"]` still has `43` items
- the plan still tracks `209` objective findings through clusters/overrides
- state still has `376` open objective defects

The bug is that none of those tracked objective IDs are currently in `queue_order`, so [`executable_objective_ids()`](/Users/user_c042661f/Documents/desloppify/desloppify/engine/_plan/schema/__init__.py#L328) returns an empty set. Then [`build_queue_snapshot()`](/Users/user_c042661f/Documents/desloppify/desloppify/engine/_work_queue/snapshot.py) sees:

- `planned_objective_count = 0`
- `objective_in_scope_count = 376`

and incorrectly falls through to `workflow_postflight`, which surfaces `workflow::communicate-score`.

So your read is correct:
- there is still real work pending
- the score item is not the right thing to show
- the actual bug is stale/tracked objective plan state suppressing action-mode execution

The fix needs to be around objective execution eligibility, not “show workflow earlier.”

{
  "patch": "*** Begin Patch\n*** Update File: /Users/user_c042661f/Documents/desloppify/desloppify/engine/_plan/schema/__init__.py\n@@\n def executable_objective_ids(\n     all_objective_ids: set[str],\n     plan: dict[str, Any] | None,\n ) -> set[str]:\n@@\n     tracked_ids = _tracked_plan_ids(plan)\n     skipped_ids = set(plan.get(\"skipped\", {}).keys())\n     tracked_objective_ids = tracked_ids & set(all_objective_ids)\n     queued_ids = {\n         issue_id\n@@\n         if issue_id not in skipped_ids\n         and not any(issue_id.startswith(prefix) for prefix in SYNTHETIC_PREFIXES)\n     }\n+    queued_objective_ids = all_objective_ids & queued_ids\n     has_active_plan_structure = bool(\n         plan.get(\"queue_order\") or plan.get(\"clusters\") or plan.get(\"overrides\")\n     )\n     if not tracked_ids:\n         return set(all_objective_ids) - skipped_ids\n     if not tracked_objective_ids:\n         return set(all_objective_ids) - skipped_ids\n-    if queued_ids:\n-        return all_objective_ids & queued_ids\n+    if queued_objective_ids:\n+        return queued_objective_ids\n+    if tracked_objective_ids:\n+        # Recover tracked objective work when queue_order has drifted to only\n+        # synthetic or non-objective items but clusters/overrides still track\n+        # live open objective IDs.\n+        return tracked_objective_ids - skipped_ids\n     if not has_active_plan_structure:\n         return set(all_objective_ids) - skipped_ids\n     return set()\n*** End Patch\n"
}

> AGENT

Yes. `plan.json` was the right place to look.

The problem was not that the plan was empty. In `reigh` the plan still had:
- `43` queue entries
- `533` overrides
- `33` clusters
- hundreds of tracked objective IDs outside `queue_order`

`next` was wrong because [`executable_objective_ids()`](/Users/user_c042661f/Documents/desloppify/desloppify/engine/_plan/schema/__init__.py#L328) only treated objective work as executable if it was still literally in `queue_order`. When `queue_order` drifted to workflow/review/subjective items, it returned no objective IDs, so `next` fell through to postflight/workflow even though real objective work was still tracked in overrides.

I fixed that recovery path in [__init__.py](/Users/user_c042661f/Documents/desloppify/desloppify/engine/_plan/schema/__init__.py) so tracked open objective work is recovered from overrides/manual cluster ownership instead of disappearing from action mode.

Result in `Documents/reigh` now:
- `desloppify next --count 10` shows real execute items again
- `desloppify plan queue` matches it
- head of queue is back to action work:
  - `auto/test_coverage`
  - logs item
  - `auto/exports`
  - unused import
  - `auto/stale_exclude`
  - `auto/security`

> AGENT

Yes. It points to a bigger issue, and `tasks/centralize-postflight-pipeline.md` still mostly makes sense, but it is incomplete.

The bigger issue is not just postflight ordering. It is split ownership of “what is live execution work” across:
- plan tracking in [schema/__init__.py](/Users/user_c042661f/Documents/desloppify/desloppify/engine/_plan/schema/__init__.py)
- phase resolution in [snapshot.py](/Users/user_c042661f/Documents/desloppify/desloppify/engine/_work_queue/snapshot.py)
- scan/import mutation paths that can leave `queue_order`, overrides, and clusters out of sync

The `reigh` bug is a good example:
- the plan still owned real objective work through overrides/clusters
- `queue_order` no longer listed it
- phase logic interpreted that as “objective work drained”
- `next` fell into postflight even though action work still existed

So `centralize-postflight-pipeline.md` is directionally right, but too narrow. Right now it assumes the main problem is “how postflight gates are ordered once execution drains.” What we just hit is one level earlier:

- the system needs a single canonical definition of executable objective work
- postflight must only begin after that canonical objective execution set is empty
- callers should not infer “drained” from `queue_order` shape alone

What I would change in the doc:
- add an explicit invariant:
  - postflight gates may run only when canonical executable objective work is empty
  - canonical objective execution must recover plan-owned work from overrides/manual cluster state, not just `queue_order`
- add a failure mode note:
  - `queue_order` can drift away from tracked plan ownership
  - lifecycle must not treat that drift as objective completion
- expand the test plan with the exact regression:
  - tracked objective work exists only via overrides/manual clusters
  - `queue_order` contains only workflow/review/subjective items
  - snapshot must still resolve `PHASE_EXECUTE`

So:
- yes, this is a bigger architectural issue
- yes, the centralization task still makes sense
- but it should be reframed around canonical execution ownership, not only postflight gate unification

If you want, I can patch the markdown with those invariants directly.

So after fixing the “score item only” bug, the queue correctly returned to `execute` phase, and `execute` phase only shows objective work. That is why `next` now shows:
- `auto/test_coverage`
- `logs`
- `auto/exports`
- `unused`
- `auto/stale_exclude`
- `auto/security`

So the real issue is:

- the thoughtful triaged clusters still exist
- but they are postflight review clusters, not executable objective clusters
- once objective work is correctly recovered, those review clusters are gated behind the execute phase

That means your reaction is right: this is not the prioritized execution queue you wanted. But the immediate reason is not that the clusters vanished. It is that they were built on `review::...` items, while the repo still has a big objective backlog.

The deeper contract problem for [centralize-postflight-pipeline.md](/Users/user_c042661f/Documents/desloppify/tasks/centralize-postflight-pipeline.md) is:
- “planned queue” is mixing two different things:
  - objective execution work
  - postflight review organization
- and the current surfaces don’t make that distinction explicit enough

So yes, that doc still makes sense, but it needs one stronger invariant:
- manual triage clusters intended to guide active execution must bind to executable objective work, not only review findings
- otherwise they will always disappear behind execute-phase objective backlog once queue recovery is correct

- backlog should not flatten “review defects” and “assessment requests” into one undifferentiated bucket

That is the part the current [centralize-postflight-pipeline.md](/Users/user_c042661f/Documents/desloppify/tasks/centralize-postflight-pipeline.md) still underspecifies. It should explicitly say:
- objective execution is separate
- postflight review defects are separate from assessment requests
- queue surfaces may show both in backlog, but they must be partitioned and labeled, not treated as one kind of work

So: your observation is correct, and it supports the broader refactor rather than contradicting it.

> DEVELOPER

nooooo it's pushing me to redo the subjectiev reviews now. Can you take a step back here? Why is tthis so conusing?

> AGENT

I’m stepping back and checking the live `reigh` state again instead of pushing another theory. The confusion is coming from multiple overlapping gates, so I want the exact current phase, triage state, and queue surfaces before saying what to change.

> AGENT

Because there were two different bugs, and I mixed them up.

What the queue model is actually doing:
- `plan.json` stores raw `queue_order` as item IDs, mostly `review::...` findings
- [plan_order.py](/Users/user_c042661f/Documents/desloppify/desloppify/engine/_work_queue/plan_order.py) collapses those review findings into the manual cluster banners you expect
- [snapshot.py](/Users/user_c042661f/Documents/desloppify/desloppify/engine/_work_queue/snapshot.py) decides which phase is active:
  - execute
  - scan
  - review postflight
  - assessment/workflow/etc.

That is why it felt slippery:
- the raw plan contains review findings
- the UI turns them into cluster banners
- subjective items still exist, but only in backlog unless the phase is `assessment_postflight`

The current live `reigh` state is now:
- `phase = review_postflight`
- active queue = the manual review/refactor clusters you wanted
- subjective items = still present in backlog, not active
- `workflow::communicate-score` = backlog, not active

So it should not be pushing you to redo subjective reviews right now.

Current `next` / `plan queue` is showing:
- `media-editing-surface-cleanup`
- `settings-and-decoder-normalization`
- `edge-function-hardening`
- `mutation-and-error-contract-alignment`
- `join-clips-and-tooling-manifest`
- `behavioral-test-backfill`
- plus 2 remaining individual review findings

The confusing part was me changing the wrong layer earlier. The correct source of truth here is:
- `queue_order` still had the planned review findings
- cluster collapse was fine
- the active phase needed to stay on review, not workflow or subjective

> DEVELOPER

but why is it postflight? we're not postflight, there are still tasks

> DEVELOPER

continue

> AGENT

Because in the current model, `postflight` does not mean “no tasks left.” It means “no planned objective/mechanical execution items left.”

Right now [plan.json](/Users/user_c042661f/Documents/reigh/.desloppify/plan.json) has:
- `1` workflow item
- `22` `review::...` items
- `20` `subjective::...` items
- `0` objective item IDs in `queue_order`

So [snapshot.py](/Users/user_c042661f/Documents/desloppify/desloppify/engine/_work_queue/snapshot.py) resolves the phase to `review_postflight`, and [plan_order.py](/Users/user_c042661f/Documents/desloppify/desloppify/engine/_work_queue/plan_order.py) collapses those `review::...` findings into the manual cluster banners you see.

That is the core confusion:
- you still have real tasks
- but the engine classifies them as planned review/refactor work, not objective execution work
- so it calls the phase `postflight`

So your objection is valid. If those clusters are supposed to be the main planned execution queue, the phase model is wrong or at least badly named. The current system is effectively saying:

- “objective queue is empty”
- “remaining work is review-derived cluster work”

If you want, I can inspect exactly why no objective IDs survived into `queue_order`, because that is probably the deeper breakage.

> DEVELOPER

can you check vs. tasks/centralize-postflight-pipeline.md - will taht simplify things and prevent these issues?

> AGENT

Partly yes.

[centralize-postflight-pipeline.md](/Users/user_c042661f/Documents/desloppify/tasks/centralize-postflight-pipeline.md) would simplify one big class of problems:
- scan/import/next deciding phase differently
- workflow items like `run-scan` or `communicate-score` surfacing at the wrong time
- postflight gates being injected in inconsistent order

That is real, and the doc is directionally correct.

But it does not fully cover the bug we just hit unless you strengthen it. The deeper issue here is not only “centralize postflight.” It is:

- define one canonical source of executable planned work
- require every surface to use that source
- only enter postflight when that canonical execution set is empty

Right now the confusion comes from three layers drifting:
- `plan.json` explicit `queue_order`
- tracked ownership via clusters/overrides
- phase resolution in [`snapshot.py`](/Users/user_c042661f/Documents/desloppify/desloppify/engine/_work_queue/snapshot.py)

That is how you end up with:
- real planned review clusters showing in `next`
- but phase named `review_postflight`
- while the user expectation is “this is still the active planned queue”

So my answer is:

- yes, the doc would help
- no, not as currently written
- it needs stronger invariants

I would add these to the doc:

- “Postflight” starts only after the canonical planned execution queue is empty.
- Canonical planned execution must be derived from explicit plan order first, not inferred opportunistically from overrides or backlog.
- Synthetic workflow items must never outrank substantive planned items in the same phase.
- Manual triage clusters that represent intended execution work need an explicit semantic status, instead of being treated as generic review backlog.

So the fix is not “more postflight centralization” alone. It is:
- centralize phase ownership
- and define what counts as the real live planned queue

Without that second part, the system will keep feeling unstable even if the phase transitions are centralized.

> AGENT

Not yet.

Findings:

- The doc still bakes in the wrong fix at [tasks/centralize-postflight-pipeline.md](/Users/user_c042661f/Documents/desloppify/tasks/centralize-postflight-pipeline.md#L46), [tasks/centralize-postflight-pipeline.md](/Users/user_c042661f/Documents/desloppify/tasks/centralize-postflight-pipeline.md#L72), and [tasks/centralize-postflight-pipeline.md](/Users/user_c042661f/Documents/desloppify/tasks/centralize-postflight-pipeline.md#L335). It says canonical execution must recover objective work from overrides/manual clusters even when `queue_order` no longer contains it. That is exactly the change that regressed `reigh` back to the mechanical auto queue. The user-visible contract is “show the actual planned items,” not “resurrect anything the plan still references.”
- The “exactly two boundary transitions” model at [tasks/centralize-postflight-pipeline.md](/Users/user_c042661f/Documents/desloppify/tasks/centralize-postflight-pipeline.md#L5) is still too coarse. In `reigh`, the live planned queue is review-derived manual clusters, and the system calling that `review_postflight` is what made everything confusing. If those clusters are the intended active queue, the model needs an explicit planned-review execution state, not just “objective execute” versus “postflight.”
- The doc is internally inconsistent about assessment work. It says assessment requests are captured in `postflight_review_items` at [tasks/centralize-postflight-pipeline.md](/Users/user_c042661f/Documents/desloppify/tasks/centralize-postflight-pipeline.md#L88), but elsewhere treats assessment work as distinct. That ambiguity is part of why the queue kept flipping between subjective reruns and review clusters.

So the direction is better, but I would not call it good yet.

What I’d change:
- Make `queue_order` the primary source of the live planned queue.
- Treat overrides/clusters as metadata/ownership unless they are explicitly promoted into the live queue.
- Add an explicit semantic state for manual clusters: active planned execution vs postflight review backlog.
- Rewrite the boundary rule to say: postflight begins only when the explicit live planned queue is empty, not when some reconstructed “canonical objective set” is empty.

That would match what you actually want the tool to do.

> AGENT

Much better. The core direction now matches the real bug.

What looks good:
- [tasks/centralize-postflight-pipeline.md#L8](/Users/user_c042661f/Documents/desloppify/tasks/centralize-postflight-pipeline.md#L8): “planned queue empties” is the right boundary, not “objective work drained.”
- [tasks/centralize-postflight-pipeline.md#L10](/Users/user_c042661f/Documents/desloppify/tasks/centralize-postflight-pipeline.md#L10): `queue_order` as the live queue and overrides/clusters as metadata is the right contract.
- [tasks/centralize-postflight-pipeline.md#L48](/Users/user_c042661f/Documents/desloppify/tasks/centralize-postflight-pipeline.md#L48): this fixes the bad resurrection idea.
- [tasks/centralize-postflight-pipeline.md#L65](/Users/user_c042661f/Documents/desloppify/tasks/centralize-postflight-pipeline.md#L65): explicit “planned review-derived work can still be execute” is the missing concept.

What still needs fixing:
- [tasks/centralize-postflight-pipeline.md#L247](/Users/user_c042661f/Documents/desloppify/tasks/centralize-postflight-pipeline.md#L247) is still wrong. `boundary_crossed: not _is_mid_cycle_scan(plan, state)` is the old objective-cycle logic. It should check explicit live planned queue emptiness, not mid-cycle/objective state.
- [tasks/centralize-postflight-pipeline.md#L179](/Users/user_c042661f/Documents/desloppify/tasks/centralize-postflight-pipeline.md#L179) is too absolute. “Triaged review findings remain `postflight_review`” conflicts with the new active-cluster model. It should say unpromoted review findings remain postflight; review findings inside `execution_status: "active"` clusters are execute work.
- [tasks/centralize-postflight-pipeline.md#L354](/Users/user_c042661f/Documents/desloppify/tasks/centralize-postflight-pipeline.md#L354) and [tasks/centralize-postflight-pipeline.md#L355](/Users/user_c042661f/Documents/desloppify/tasks/centralize-postflight-pipeline.md#L355) still describe the wrong regression test. That was the overcompensating fix we already rejected. Those tests should now assert “do not resurrect from overrides/clusters when not in `queue_order`.”

So: yes, it’s now mostly right, but those 3 spots still need to be aligned or the implementation will drift back into the same confusion.
